that type continuations preserve subtyping;
and
(ii)
the remainder of the context is unaffected by the communication. ∎

Theorem 2

Assume
\emptyset\,;{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}\vdash P
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}
safe and
P\equiv{\bigm|_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{q}}}\in I}\!P_{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{q}}}}
where each
P_{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{q}}}
is either
\bm{0}
(up-to

\equiv
), or only plays role
{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{q}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.8,0.9}s}
.
Then,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\rightarrow}
implies
\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}^{\prime}},P^{\prime}
s.t.
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\rightarrow}{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}^{\prime}}
,
P\rightarrow^{*}P^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}^{\prime}}\vdash P^{\prime}
, where
P^{\prime}\equiv{\bigm|_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{q}}}\in I}\!P_{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{q}}}^{\prime}}
and each
P_{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{q}}}^{\prime}
is either
\bm{0}
(up-to
\equiv
), or only plays role
{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{q}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.8,0.9}s}
.

Proof
(Sketch)

We start by observing that if
\varphi({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\rightarrow}
, then we can split out of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}
either

(i)
a selection and branch type; or
(ii)
a selection and replicated branch type.
Proceeding in a similar manner for both cases, we infer the shape of
P
based on the split types.
It follows that
P
can reduce via
Figure

2
(or
Figure

2
/
Figure

2
in case
(ii)
), possibly after some applications of
Figure

2
,
Figure

2
, and
Figure

2
; and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}
can reduce such that the process reduction remains typable.
Lastly, we show that the process reductum retains the structure of the assumptions for session fidelity (by rule
Figure

2
and since
Definition

7
requires its properties to hold on all subterms).
We note that for replicated communication, a process
P_{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{p}}}
can take the shape
P_{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{p}}}\equiv{\bigm|_{i\in I}\!P_{i}^{\prime}}
where each
P_{i}^{\prime}
is either
\bm{0}
(up-to
\equiv
), or only plays role
{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{p}}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.8,0.9}s}
;
i.e.,
a process playing a single role can consist of multiple parallel processes that all play the same role.
In this case we first show that
P_{\color[rgb]{1,0.1,0.14}{\bm{p}}}
is typed under a runtime type

{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U}
, then proceed in a similar manner as before.

Appendix 0.D
Unfolding

Below we present our definitions for type and context unfolding; they are mostly standard, we only adapt type unfolding to cater for parallel types.

Definition 12
(Type unfolding)

The one-step unfolding of a type
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U}
, written
\textsf{unf}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U})
, is given by:

\textsf{unf}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\mu\textnormal{{t}}.S})={\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}S}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\mu\textnormal{{t}}.S}/{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\textnormal{{t}}}\}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \textsf{unf}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}S\,|\,U})=\textsf{unf}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}S}){\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\,|\,}\textsf{unf}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U})\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \textsf{unf}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}S})={\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}S}\ \ \ \ \textit{if }{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}S}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\mu\textnormal{{t}}.S^{\prime}}

The n-steps unfolding of a type
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U}
, written
\textsf{unf}^{n}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U})
, is given by:

\textsf{unf}^{0}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U})={\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \textsf{unf}^{m+1}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U})=\textsf{unf}(\textsf{unf}^{m}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U}))

The complete unfolding of a type
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U}
, written
\textsf{unf}^{*}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U})
, is defined as:

\textsf{unf}^{*}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U})=\textsf{unf}^{n}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U})\ \ \ \ \textit{for the smallest $n$ s.t. }\textsf{unf}^{n}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U})=\textsf{unf}^{n+1}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U})

Definition 13
(Context unfolding, as in
[
25
]
definition K.1)

The set of unfoldings of a type context
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}
, written
\textsf{unf}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma})
, is defined below (where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U}/c\}
is a mapping update):

\textsf{unf}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma})=\textstyle\bigcup_{c\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}:}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}\{\textsf{unf}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}U})/c\}\}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \textit{extends to sets of contexts as}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \textsf{unf}(\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}_{i}}\}_{i\in I})=\textstyle\bigcup_{i\in I}\textsf{unf}({\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}{\color[rgb]{0,0.21,0.79}\Gamma}_{i}})

Given a set of contexts
\xi
, the closure of its unfoldings, written
\textsf{unf}*(\xi)
, is defined as:

\textsf{unf}^{*}(\xi)=\textsf{lfix}(\lambda\xi^{\prime}.\xi\cup\xi^{\prime}\cup\textsf{unf}(\xi\cup\xi^{\prime}))\ \ \ \ \textit{where }\textsf{lfix}\textit{ is the least fixed point of its argument}
